## Waveform (we miss you)

Path: `MUSICIANS/D/Danko_Tomas/Waveform_we_miss_you.sid`

Author: Tomas Danko · Release/year: 1997 Tomas Danko

PSID; BASIC flag clear; 1 subtunes. Complete-file MD5: `3ba4ccb43609e43f7bc1464ca572b421`; SHA-256: `fe7f1916ca7107cb7c5b723e765c8e21f17b3b8d07fe59148fec0cdcb242ef70`.

| Subtune | Seconds | Evidence | Reference RMS; writes | Neither | K-only | B-only |
|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2 | pcm-difference-with-similar-write-sequence | 1.739e-2; 2737 | ΔRMS 3.935e-3; writes 2737; prefix 2737; drift 13; derived | identical PCM; writes 2737; prefix 2737; drift 0; derived | ΔRMS 3.935e-3; writes 2737; prefix 2737; drift 13 |

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/D/Danko_Tomas/
> COMMENT: Tomas Danko's own comments are indicated by (TD).
>
> COMMENT: "I was hanging out on IRC on a regular basis with Waveform. Then he
>          disappeared. I later found out he had terminal cancer.
>          This crushed me to no end. At work I started playing these harmonies
>          on a keyboard (I owned a music store at the time) and it evolved
>          into a song.
>          I wanted him to hear the track to support him in his struggle. Memory
>          eludes me but I do believe he got the word I was making a SID for him
>          and he liked that.
>          He never got to hear the final version. I was completely crushed over
>          this for quite some time.
>          Emulation won't give this track a fair chance, I'm kicking the shit
>          out of old school analog synthesis knowledge and using very slow
>          filter and ringmodulation etc to slowly move through harmonics
>          matching the octaves in sync with the patterns so that the song opens
>          up slowly. This is lost in translation on any emulator." (TD)
